<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Isaac wants to make $36$ cupcakes. How much flour should he use?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour used $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{cupcakes} : 180 \ \text{grams}       &= 36 \ \text{cupcakes} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {180}                                &= \dfrac {36} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 36 \times 180 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {36 \times 180} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 12 \times 180} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {12} \times 180} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 180 \\\\
a                                               &= 540 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour used $= 540 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratios, 

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cupcakes}                 &:&     \text{flour} \\\\
= & 12                              &:&     180 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 12  \times 3                    &:&     180 \times 3 \\\\
= & 36                              &:&     540 \ \text{g}                   
\end{array}
$

Flour used $= 540 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$540 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of flour used $= a$
<emptyline>

12 \times a                                     &= 36 \times 180 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {36 \times 180} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 12 \times 180} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {12} \times 180} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 180 \\\\
a                                               &= 540 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Flour used $= 540 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cupcakes}                 &:&     \text{flour} \\\\

= & 12  \times 3                    &:&     180 \times 3 \\\\
= & 36                              &:&     540 \ \text{g}

<emptyline>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Lucy has $1  \ \text{kg}$ of caster sugar.

Given that she had plenty of all the other ingredients, how many cupcakes can she make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of cupcakes she can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
200 \ \text{g} : 12 \ \text{cupcakes}           &= 1 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{cupcakes} \\\\
\dfrac {200 \ \text{g}} {12}                    &= \dfrac {1 \ \text{kg}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac {200 \ \text{g}} {12}                    &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
200 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 12} {200} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {200 \times 5 \times 12} {200} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {200} \times 5 \times 12} {\cancel {200}} \\\\
a                                               &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

Number of cupcakes $= 60$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratios,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{caster sugar}             &:& \text{cupcakes} \\\\
= & 200 \ \text{g}                  &:& 12 \\\\
= & 200  \times 5                   &:& 12 \times 5 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{g}                 &:& 60 \\\\                   
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                   &:& 60
\end{array}
$

Number of cupcakes $= 60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of cupcakes she can make $= a$

\dfrac {200 \ \text{g}} {12}                    &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
200 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 12} {200} \\\\

a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {200} \times 5 \times 12} {\cancel {200}} \\\\
a                                               &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of cupcakes $= 60$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

= & 1000 \ \text{g}                 &:& 60 \\\\                   
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                   &:& 60
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

There is $6 \ \text{g}$ of sugar in every $10 \ \text{g}$ of jam. How much sugar is in $500 \ \text{g}$ of jam?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of sugar $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
10 \ \text{grams} : 6 \ \text{grams}            &= 500 \ \text{grams} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac {10} {6}                                 &= \dfrac {500} {a} \\\\
10 \times a                                     &= 500 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {500 \times 6} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {50 \cancel 0 \times 6} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 50 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 300 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Sugar $= 300 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{jam}                              &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 10 \ \text{g}                           &:& 6 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 10 \times 50                            &:& 6 \times 50 \\\\
= & 500 \ \text{g}                          &:& 300 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Sugar $= 300 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$300 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of sugar $= a$
<emptyline>

a                                               &= \dfrac {500 \times 6} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {50 \cancel 0 \times 6} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 50 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 300 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Sugar $= 300 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>

= & 500 \ \text{g}                          &:& 300 \ \text{g}
\end{array}

<div class='question question'>

There is $5.5\ \text{g}$ of sugar in every $10\ \text{g}$ of jam. How much sugar is in $600\ \text{g}$ of jam?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of sugar $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
10 \ \text{grams} : 5.5 \ \text{grams}          &= 600 \ \text{grams} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac {10} {5.5}                               &= \dfrac {600} {a} \\\\
10 \times a                                     &= 600 \times 5.5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {600 \times 5.5} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {60 \cancel 0 \times 5.5} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 60 \times 5.5 \\\\
a                                               &= 330 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Sugar $= 330 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{jam}                              &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 10 \ \text{g}                           &:& 5.5 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 10 \times 60                            &:& 5.5 \times 60 \\\\
= & 600 \ \text{g}                          &:& 330 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Sugar $= 330 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$330 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

a                                               &= \dfrac {600 \times 5.5} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {60 \cancel 0 \times 5.5} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 60 \times 5.5 \\\\
a                                               &= 330 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Sugar $= 330 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{jam}                              &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 10 \ \text{g}                           &:& 5.5 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 10 \times 60                            &:& 5.5 \times 60 \\\\
= & 600 \ \text{g}                          &:& 330 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Sugar $= 330 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>

<li>
tratio

</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A recipe for $4$ people needs $150 \ \text{g}$ of flour. How much flour would you need to make the same recipe for $6$ people?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour used $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{people} : 150 \ \text{grams}              &= 6 \ \text{people} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{4} {150}                                 &= \dfrac {6} {a} \\\\
4 \times a                                      &= 6 \times 150 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 150} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 2 \times 75} {2 \times 2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times 75} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 75 \\\\
a                                               &= 225 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 225 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{people}                           &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& 150 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {150} {4} \\\\
= & 1 \times 6                              &:& \dfrac {150 \times 6} {4} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 75 \times 3 \times 2} {2 \times 2} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75 \times 3 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& 75 \times 3 \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& 225 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 225 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$225 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of flour used $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{people} : 150 \ \text{grams}              &= 6 \ \text{people} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\

a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 150} {4} \\\\

a                                               &= 3 \times 75 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Flour needed $= 225 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>

<div class='papername'>
<p>dulwich-college--11-plus--maths--9999--specimen-paper-h:1:14</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='topics'>

<li>
trecipe
</li>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Shortcrust pastry is made using flour and fat in the ratio $2 : 1$.

How many grams of flour are needed to make $900 \ \text{grams}$ of shortcrust pastry?  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Flour needed}              &= \dfrac {2} {3} \times 900 \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 900} {3} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 300 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 300 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
                                 &= 2 \times 300  \\\\
                                 &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the quantity of flour needed $= a$

Quantity of fat needed $= 900 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{grams} : 2 \ \text{grams}                     &= (900 - a) \ \text{grams} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{1} {2}                                   &= \dfrac {900 - a} {a} \\\\
a                                               &= (900 - a) \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 1800 - 2a \\\\
a + 2a                                          &= 1800 \\\\
3a                                              &= 1800 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1800} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 600} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 600} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour $= 600 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pastry}                       &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 2 + 1                               &:& 2 \\\\
= & 3                                   &:& 2 \\\\
= & 3 \times 300                        &:& 2 \times 300 \\\\
= & 900 \ \text{g}                      &:& 600 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour $= 600 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Flour needed}              &= \dfrac {2} {3} \times 900 \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 900} {3} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 300 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                 &= \dfrac {2 \times 300 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
                                 &= 2 \times 300  \\\\
                                 &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the quantity of flour needed $= a$
<emptyline>
Quantity of fat needed $= 900 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{grams} : 2 \ \text{grams}                     &= (900 - a) \ \text{grams} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{1} {2}                                   &= \dfrac {900 - a} {a} \\\\
a                                               &= (900 - a) \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 1800 - 2a \\\\
a + 2a                                          &= 1800 \\\\
3a                                              &= 1800 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1800} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 600} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 600} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Flour $= 600 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pastry}                       &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 2 + 1                               &:& 2 \\\\
= & 3                                   &:& 2 \\\\
= & 3 \times 300                        &:& 2 \times 300 \\\\
= & 900 \ \text{g}                      &:& 600 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Flour $= 600 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g3 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>773EFA22-B82F-48ED-9E20-221064897434</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>forest-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1:1:30</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g3</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
trecipe
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A receipe of cake needs $200 \ \text{g}$ of flour.
Issac has $250 \ \text{g}$ of flour.
How many cakes could he make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of cakes he can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
200 \ \text{g} : 12 \ \text{cakes}              &= 250 \ \text{g} : a \ \text{cakes} \\\\
\dfrac {200} {12}                               &= \dfrac {250} {a} \\\\
200 \times a                                    &= 250 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {250 \times 12} {200} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {50 \times 5 \times 4 \times 3} {50 \times 4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {50} \times 5 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel {50} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 5 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

Maximum cakes $= 15$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flour}                            &:& \text{cakes} \\\\
= & 200 \ \text{g}                          &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {12} {200} \\\\
= & 1 \times 250                            &:& \dfrac {12 \times 250} {200} \\\\
= & 250                                     &:& \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 50 \times 5} {50 \times 4} \\\\
= & 250                                     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times \cancel {50} \times 5} {\cancel {50} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
= & 250                                     &:& 3 \times 5 \\\\
= & 250 \ \text{g}                          &:& 15
\end{array}
$

Maximum cakes $= 15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\end{aligned}

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Natasha made $60$ Chocolate Brownies. How much butter did she use?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of butter used $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
15 \ \text{brownies} : 110 \ \text{grams}       &= 60 \ \text{brownies} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac {15} {110}                               &= \dfrac {60} {a} \\\\
15 \times a                                     &= 60 \times 110 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {60 \times 110} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {15 \times 4 \times 110} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {15} \times 4 \times 110} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
a                                               &= 4 \times 110 \\\\
a                                               &= 440 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Butter used $= 440 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{chocolate brownies}               &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 15                                      &:& 110 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 15 \times 4                             &:& 110 \times 4 \\\\
= & 60                                      &:& 440 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Butter used $= 440 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$440 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of butter used $= a$
<emptyline>

a                                               &= \dfrac {15 \times 4 \times 110} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {15} \times 4 \times 110} {\cancel {15}} \\\\

a                                               &= 440 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Stella has $150 \ \text{grams}$ of flour and $300 \ \text{grams}$ of nuts, 
and plenty of the other ingredients. She makes as many Chocolate Brownies as she can.

Which ingredient will she run out of, flour or nuts? Show how you decide.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using part to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flour}                            &:& \text{nuts} \\\\
= & 50 \ \text{g}                           &:& 120 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 50 \times 3                             &:& 120 \times 3 \\\\
= & 150 \ \text{g}                          &:& 360 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

For $150 \ \text{g}$ of flour, Stella need $360 \ \text{g}$ of nuts.

So she will run out of nuts first.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Nuts

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using part to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flour}                            &:& \text{nuts} \\\\
= & 50 \ \text{g}                           &:& 120 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 50 \times 3                             &:& 120 \times 3 \\\\
= & 150 \ \text{g}                          &:& 360 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
For $150 \ \text{g}$ of flour, Stella need $360 \ \text{g}$ of nuts.
<emptyline>
So she will run out of nuts first.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Nuts
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Pauline wants to make $10$ Chocolate Brownies, how much sugar will she need? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of sugar used $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
15 \ \text{brownies} : 225 \ \text{grams}       &= 10 \ \text{brownies} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac {15} {225}                               &= \dfrac {10} {a} \\\\
15 \times a                                     &= 10 \times 225 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 225} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 15 \times 15} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {15} \times 15} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 15 \\\\
a                                               &= 150 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Sugar needed $= 150 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{chocolate brownies}               &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 15                                      &:& 225 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {225} {15} \\\\
= & 1 \times 10                             &:& \dfrac {225 \times 10} {15} \\\\
= & 10                                      &:& \dfrac {15 \times 15 \times 10} {15} \\\\
= & 10                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel {15} \times 15 \times 10} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
= & 10                                      &:& 15 \times 10 \\\\
= & 10                                      &:& 150 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Sugar needed $= 150 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$150 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of sugar used $= a$

$

a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 15 \times 15} {15} \\\\

a                                               &= 10 \times 15 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

For $8$ people I would need $ \ldots $ eggs and  $ \ldots \ \text{ml} $ of milk.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{people}   &:& \text{eggs}                                                                  &:& \text{milk} \\\\
= & 6               &:& 3                                                                            &:& 150 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1               &:& \dfrac {3} {6}                                                               &:& \dfrac {150} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 8      &:& \dfrac {3 \times 8} {6}                                                      &:& \dfrac {150 \times 8} {6} \\\\
= & 8               &:& \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 4} {2 \times 3}                                    &:& \dfrac {6 \times 25 \times 8} {6} \\\\
= & 8               &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2 \times 4} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 3}    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 25 \times 8} {\cancel 6} \\\\
= & 8               &:& 4                                                                            &:& 25 \times 8 \\\\
= & 8               &:& 4                                                                            &:& 200 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Eggs $=$ $4 \ \text{eggs}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Milk $=$ $200 \ \text{ml}$

<div class='question subquestion'>

If instead I wanted to make the pudding for $9$ people then $\ldots$

For 9 people I would need $\ldots \ \text{g}$ of sultanas and  $\ldots \ \text{g}$ of butter.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{people}   &:& \text{sultanas}                                      &:& \text{butter} \\\\
= & 6               &:& 90 \ \text{g}                                        &:& 45 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1               &:& \dfrac {90} {6}                                      &:& \dfrac {45} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 9      &:& \dfrac {90 \times 9} {6}                             &:& \dfrac {45 \times 9} {6} \\\\
= & 9               &:& \dfrac {6 \times 15 \times 9} {6}                    &:& \dfrac {3 \times 15 \times 9} {3 \times 2} \\\\

= & 9               &:& 15 \times 9                                          &:& \dfrac {675} {10} \\\\
= & 9               &:& 135 \ \text{g}                                       &:& 67.5 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Later, I find the recipe for the same pudding in a recipe book, but this version calls for $5$ eggs.

How many people is this $5$ egg version intended to serve? It serves $\ldots$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of people that will be served $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{eggs} : 6 \ \text{people}             &= 5 \ \text{eggs} : a \ \text{people} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {6}                                  &= \dfrac {5} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 6} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 5 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Number of people served $= 10$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{eggs}                             &:& \text{people} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 6 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {6} {3} \\\\
= & 1 \times 5                              &:& \dfrac {6 \times 5} {3} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 5} {3} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 2 \times 5} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 2 \times 5 \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 10
\end{array}
$

Number of people served $= 10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of people that will be served $= a$
<emptyline>

a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 5 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of people served $= 10$
</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,

= & 5                                       &:& 10
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of people served $= 10$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In the $5$ egg version, you would need to use $\ldots \ \text{g}$ of flour.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour used $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{eggs} : 240 \ \text{grams}                &= 5 \ \text{eggs} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {240}                                &= \dfrac {5} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 5 \times 240 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 240} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 80 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 80 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 5 \times 80 \\\\
a                                               &= 400 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour used $= 400 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{eggs}                             &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 240 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {240} {3} \\\\
= & 1 \times 5                              &:& \dfrac {240 \times 5} {3} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& \dfrac {3 \times 80 \times 5} {3} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 80 \times 5} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 80 \times 5 \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 400 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour used $= 400 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$400 \ \text{g}$

</div>

How much of each ingredient is needed to make $18$ pancakes?

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pancakes} &:& \text{flour}                &:& \text{eggs} &:& \text{milk} \\\\
= & 6               &:& 120 \ \text{g}              &:& 3           &:& 210 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 6 \times 3      &:& 120 \times 3                &:& 3 \times 3  &:& 210 \times 3 \\\\
= & 18              &:& 360 \ \text{g}              &:& 9           &:& 630 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{pancakes} &:& \text{flour}                                            &:& \text{eggs}                                                                     &:& \text{milk} \\\\
= & 6               &:& 120 \ \text{g}                                          &:& 3                                                                               &:& 210 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1               &:& \dfrac {120} {6}                                        &:& \dfrac {3} {6}                                                                  &:& \dfrac {210} {6} \\\\
= & 8               &:& \dfrac {120 \times 8} {6}                               &:& \dfrac {3 \times 8} {6}                                                         &:& \dfrac {210 \times 8} {6} \\\\
= & 8               &:& \dfrac {6 \times 20 \times 8} {6}                       &:& \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 4} {2 \times 3}                                       &:& \dfrac {3 \times 70 \times 2 \times 4} {2 \times 3} \\\\
= & 8               &:& \dfrac {\cancel{6} \times 20 \times 8} {\cancel{6}}     &:& \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times \cancel{2} \times 4} {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{3}}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 70 \times \cancel{2} \times 4} {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{3}} \\\\
= & 8               &:& 160 \ \text{g}                                          &:& 4                                                                               &:& 280 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='answer'>

Plain flour $=$ $160 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Eggs $=$ $4$

</div>

<div class='question question'>

A recipe for making $12$ cupcakes includes the following ingredients:

Sugar $120 \ \text{g}$ 

Eggs $3$

Butter $150 \ \text{g}$ 

Flour $180 \ \text{g}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate the quantities needed to make $16$ cupcakes.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cupcakes}   &:& \text{flour}                                                                            &:& \text{eggs}                                                                 &:& \text{butter}                                                                         &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 12                &:& 180 \ \text{g}                                                                          &:& 3                                                                           &:& 150 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 120 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {180} {12}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {3} {12}                                                             &:& \dfrac {150} {12}                                                                     &:& \dfrac {120} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 16       &:& \dfrac {180 \times 16} {12}                                                             &:& \dfrac {3 \times 16} {12}                                                   &:& \dfrac {150 \times 16} {12}                                                           &:& \dfrac {120 \times 16} {12} \\\\
= & 16                &:& \dfrac {3 \times 60 \times 4 \times 4} {4 \times 3}                                     &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 4} {4 \times 3}                                   &:& \dfrac {50 \times 3 \times 4 \times 4} {4 \times 3}                                   &:& \dfrac {12 \times 10 \times 16} {12} \\\\
= & 16                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 60 \times 4 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3}     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3}   &:& \dfrac {50 \times \cancel 3 \times 4 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 10 \times 16} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
= & 16                &:& 60 \times 4                                                                             &:& 4                                                                           &:& 50 \times 4                                                                           &:& 10 \times 16 \\\\
= & 16                &:& 240 \ \text{g}                                                                          &:& 4                                                                           &:& 200 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 160 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Sugar $=$ $160 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Eggs $=$ $4$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Butter $=$ $200 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Flour $=$ $240 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cupcakes}   &:& \text{flour}                                                                            &:& \text{eggs}                                                                 &:& \text{butter}                                                                         &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 12                &:& 180 \ \text{g}                                                                          &:& 3                                                                           &:& 150 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 120 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {180} {12}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {3} {12}                                                             &:& \dfrac {150} {12}                                                                     &:& \dfrac {120} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 16       &:& \dfrac {180 \times 16} {12}                                                             &:& \dfrac {3 \times 16} {12}                                                   &:& \dfrac {150 \times 16} {12}                                                           &:& \dfrac {120 \times 16} {12} \\\\
= & 16                &:& \dfrac {3 \times 60 \times 4 \times 4} {4 \times 3}                                     &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 4} {4 \times 3}                                   &:& \dfrac {50 \times 3 \times 4 \times 4} {4 \times 3}                                   &:& \dfrac {12 \times 10 \times 16} {12} \\\\
= & 16                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 60 \times 4 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3}     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3}   &:& \dfrac {50 \times \cancel 3 \times 4 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 3}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 10 \times 16} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
= & 16                &:& 60 \times 4                                                                             &:& 4                                                                           &:& 50 \times 4                                                                           &:& 10 \times 16 \\\\
= & 16                &:& 240 \ \text{g}                                                                          &:& 4                                                                           &:& 200 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 160 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Eggs</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$200 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Flour</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$240 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If you have plenty of all of the other ingredients but only $7$ eggs, how many cupcakes can you make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of cupcakes we can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{eggs} : 12 \ \text{cupcakes}          &= 7 \ \text{eggs} : a \ \text{cupcakes} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {12}                                 &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 7 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 12} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 4 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 4 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 7 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 28
\end{aligned}
$

Maximum cupcakes $= 28$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratios,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{eggs}       &:& \text{cupcakes} \\\\
= & 3                 &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
= & 1 \times 7        &:& \dfrac {12 \times 7} {3} \\\\
= & 7                 &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 7} {3} \\\\
= & 7                 &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4 \times 7} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 7                 &:& 4 \times 7 \\\\
= & 7                 &:& 28 
\end{array}
$

Maximum cupcakes $= 28$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$28$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of cupcakes we can make $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{eggs} : 12 \ \text{cupcakes}          &= 7 \ \text{eggs} : a \ \text{cupcakes} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {12}                                 &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 7 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 12} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 4 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 4 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 7 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 28
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Maximum cupcakes $= 28$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratios,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{eggs}       &:& \text{cupcakes} \\\\

= & 7                 &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 7} {3} \\\\
= & 7                 &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4 \times 7} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 7                 &:& 4 \times 7 \\\\
= & 7                 &:& 28 
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Maximum cupcakes $= 28$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$28$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write out the ingredients you would need to make $10$ flapjacks.

$\ldots$ grams of margarine

$\ldots$ grams of oats

$\ldots$ grams of sugar grams of flour

$\ldots$ tablespoons of syrup 


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flapjacks}                          &:& \text{margarine}                                          &:& \text{oats}                                     &:& \text{sugar}                                                &:& \text{flour}                                &:& \text{syrup} \\\\
= & 20                                        &:& 200 \ \text{g}                                            &:& 250 \ \text{g}                                  &:& 200 \ \text{g}                                              &:& 100 \ \text{g}                              &:& 3 \ \text{tablespoons} \\\\
= & \dfrac {20} {2}                           &:& \dfrac {200} {2}                                          &:& \dfrac {250} {2}                                &:& \dfrac {200} {2}                                            &:& \dfrac {100} {2}                            &:& \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {2 \times 10} {2}                  &:& \dfrac {100 \times 2} {2}                                 &:& \dfrac {125 \times 2} {2}                       &:& \dfrac {100 \times 2} {2}                                   &:& \dfrac {50 \times 2} {2}                    &:& \dfrac {3 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
= & \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 10} {\cancel 2}  &:& \dfrac {100 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2}                 &:& \dfrac {125 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2}       &:& \dfrac {100 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2}                   &:& \dfrac {50 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2}    &:& \dfrac {15} {10} \\\\
= & 10                                        &:& 100 \ \text{g}                                            &:& 125 \ \text{g}                                  &:& 100 \ \text{g}                                              &:& 50 \ \text{g}                               &:& 1.5 \ \text{tablespoons}
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='answer'>

Margarine $=$ $100 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Oats $=$ $125 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Sugar $=$ $100 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Flour $=$ $50 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Syrup $=$ $1.5 \ \text{tablespoons}$

</div>
</div>

= & \dfrac {2 \times 10} {2}                  &:& \dfrac {100 \times 2} {2}                                 &:& \dfrac {125 \times 2} {2}                       &:& \dfrac {100 \times 2} {2}                                   &:& \dfrac {50 \times 2} {2}                    &:& \dfrac {3 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Kelly needs to make $50$ flapjacks for a party. 
How much flour does she need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
20 \ \text{flapjacks} : 100 \ \text{grams}          &= 50 \ \text{flapjacks} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac {20} {100}                               &= \dfrac {50} {a} \\\\
20 \times a                                     &= 50 \times 100 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {50 \times 100} {20} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {50 \times 20 \times 5} {20} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {20} \times 5} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
a                                               &= 50 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 250 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 250 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flapjacks}      &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 20                    &:& 100 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                     &:& \dfrac {100} {20} \\\\
= & 1 \times 50           &:& \dfrac {100 \times 50} {20} \\\\
= & 50                    &:& \dfrac {20 \times 5 \times 50} {20} \\\\
= & 50                    &:& \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 5 \times 50} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
= & 50                    &:& 5 \times 50 \\\\
= & 50                    &:& 250 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 250 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$250 \ \text {g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of flour needed $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

a                                               &= \dfrac {50 \times 100} {20} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {50 \times 20 \times 5} {20} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {20} \times 5} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
a                                               &= 50 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 250 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Flour needed $= 250 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>

= & 50                    &:& 5 \times 50 \\\\
= & 50                    &:& 250 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Flour needed $= 250 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$250 \ \text {g}$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

To make healthier flapjacks, you can use $\dfrac{3}{4}$ of the recommended amount of sugar.

How much sugar would you use to make $20$ of these healthier flapjacks?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amount of sugar}        &= \dfrac {3} {4} \times 200 \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {3 \times 200} {4} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 50} {4} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 4 \times 50} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                              &= 150 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$150 \ \text {g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                              &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 4 \times 50} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                              &= 150 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A recipe for making $12$ large chocolate chunk cookies includes the following ingredients:

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2008--group-1/section-1-question-13-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2008--group-1/section-1-question-13-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2008--group-1/section-1-question-13-00.png")


$300 \ \text{g}$ plain chocolate

$100 \ \text{g}$ sugar

$85 \ \text{g}$ butter

$1$ large egg

$\dfrac{1}{2}$ teaspoon vanilla extract

$100 \ \text{g}$ self-raising flour

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If Jamie makes $30$ cookies, how much plain chocolate will he need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of chocolate needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{cookies} : 300 \ \text{grams}        &= 30 \ \text{cookies} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {300}                                &= \dfrac {30} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 30 \times 300 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {30 \times 300} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {30 \times 12 \times 25} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel {12} \times 25} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                               &= 30 \times 25 \\\\
a                                               &= 750 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Chocolate needed $= 750 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cookies}                          &:& \text{chocolate} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 300 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {300} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 30                             &:& \dfrac {300 \times 30} {12} \\\\
= & 30                                      &:& \dfrac {12 \times 25 \times 30} {12} \\\\
= & 30                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 25 \times 30} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
= & 30                                      &:& 25 \times 30 \\\\
= & 30                                      &:& 750 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Chocolate needed $= 750 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$750 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of chocolate needed $= a$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jamie calculates that the cost of making $30$ cookies is $\pounds 5.10$. If he sells all $30$ cookies at $26 \ \text{p}$ each, how much profit will he make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Selling price}                    &= 30 \times 26 \\\\
                                        &= 780 \ \text{p} \\\\
\text{Profit}                           &= 780 \ \text{p} - \pounds 5.10 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds  7.80 - \pounds 5.10 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds  2.70
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds  2.70$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The amount of each ingredient needed to  make $8$ cakes is given in the table below.



|**ingredients**|**$8$ cakes**   |**$12$ cakes**      |
|:-------------:|:--------------:|:------------------:|
|flour          |$240 \text{g}$  |$\ldots \text{g}$   |
|eggs           |$4$             |  $\ldots$          |
|sugar          |$6$ tablespoons |$\ldots$ tablespoons| 
|butter         |$150 \text{g}$  | $\ldots \text{g}$  |         

Complete the table to show how much of each ingredient is needed to make $12$ cakes.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cakes}      &:& \text{flour}                                           &:& \text{eggs}                                                                 &:& \text{butter}                                                                         &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 8                 &:& 240 \ \text{g}                                         &:& 4                                                                           &:& 150 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 6 \ \text{tablespoons} \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {240} {8}                                       &:& \dfrac {4} {8}                                                              &:& \dfrac {150} {8}                                                                      &:& \dfrac {6} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 12       &:& \dfrac {240 \times 12} {8}                             &:& \dfrac {4 \times 12} {8}                                                    &:& \dfrac {150 \times 12} {8}                                                            &:& \dfrac {6 \times 12} {8} \\\\
= & 12                &:& \dfrac {8 \times 30 \times 12} {8}                     &:& \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 6} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {75 \times 2 \times 4 \times 3} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 4 \times 3} {4 \times 2} \\\\
= & 12                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 30 \times 12} {\cancel 8}     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {75 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 12                &:& 30 \times 12                                           &:& 6                                                                           &:& 75 \times 3                                                                           &:& 3 \times 3 \\\\
= & 12                &:& 360 \ \text{g}                                         &:& 6                                                                           &:& 225 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 9 \ \text{tablespoons}
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Flour $=$ $360 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Eggs $=$ $6$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Butter $=$ $225 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Sugar $=$ $9 \ \text{tablespoons}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cakes}      &:& \text{flour}                                           &:& \text{eggs}                                                                 &:& \text{butter}                                                                         &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 8                 &:& 240 \ \text{g}                                         &:& 4                                                                           &:& 150 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 6 \ \text{tablespoons} \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {240} {8}                                       &:& \dfrac {4} {8}                                                              &:& \dfrac {150} {8}                                                                      &:& \dfrac {6} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 12       &:& \dfrac {240 \times 12} {8}                             &:& \dfrac {4 \times 12} {8}                                                    &:& \dfrac {150 \times 12} {8}                                                            &:& \dfrac {6 \times 12} {8} \\\\
= & 12                &:& \dfrac {8 \times 30 \times 12} {8}                     &:& \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 6} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {75 \times 2 \times 4 \times 3} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 4 \times 3} {4 \times 2} \\\\
= & 12                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 30 \times 12} {\cancel 8}     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {75 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 12                &:& 30 \times 12                                           &:& 6                                                                           &:& 75 \times 3                                                                           &:& 3 \times 3 \\\\
= & 12                &:& 360 \ \text{g}                                         &:& 6                                                                           &:& 225 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 9 \ \text{tablespoons}
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

A recipe for making $8$ portions of cheesy pasta requires the following ingredients:

$500 \ \text{grams}$ pasta

$150 \ \text{grams}$ cheese

$20 \ \text{millilitres}$ cream

$8$ sundried tomatoes

</div>

</div>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>

</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much cheese is needed to make $4$ portions of cheesy pasta?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of cheese needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
8 \ \text{portions} : 150 \ \text{grams}            &= 4 \ \text{portions} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{8} {150}                                 &= \dfrac {4} {a} \\\\
8 \times a                                      &= 4 \times 150 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 150} {8} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 75} {2 \times 4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2 \times 75} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 75 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Cheese needed $= 75 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{portions}                         &:& \text{cheese} \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& 150 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {150} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 4                              &:& \dfrac {150 \times 4} {8} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 75 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& 75 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Cheese needed $= 75 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of cheese needed $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
8 \ \text{portions} : 150 \ \text{grams}            &= 4 \ \text{portions} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{8} {150}                                 &= \dfrac {4} {a} \\\\
8 \times a                                      &= 4 \times 150 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 150} {8} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 75} {2 \times 4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2 \times 75} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 75 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cheese needed $= 75 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{portions}                         &:& \text{cheese} \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& 150 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {150} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 4                              &:& \dfrac {150 \times 4} {8} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 75 \times 4} {2 \times 4} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
= & 4                                       &:& 75 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Cheese needed $= 75 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$75 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Mrs. Smith is making $20$ portions of cheesy pasta.

Complete the list below to show the amount of each ingredient which Mrs. Smith needs.



$\ldots \ldots \text{grams}$ pasta

$\ldots \ldots \text{grams}$ cheese

$\ldots \ldots \text{millilitres}$ cream

$20$ sundried tomatoes

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{portions}       &:& \text{pasta}                                                                           &:& \text{cheese}                                                                          &:& \text{cream}                                                                          &:& \text{tomatoes} \\\\
= & 8                     &:& 500 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 150 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 20 \ \text{ml}                                                                        &:& 8 \\\\
= & 1                     &:& \dfrac {500} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {150} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {20} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {8} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 20           &:& \dfrac {500 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {150 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {20 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {8 \times 20} {8} \\\\
= & 20                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 250 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 75 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 10 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 20} {\cancel 8} \\\\
= & 20                    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 250 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 10 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& 20 \\\\
= & 20                    &:& 250 \times 5                                                                           &:& 75 \times 5                                                                            &:& 10 \times 5                                                                           &:& 20 \\\\
= & 20                    &:& 1250 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 375 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 50 \ \text{ml}                                                                        &:& 20
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Pasta $=$ $1250 \ \text{g}$

</div>

= & 8                     &:& 500 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 150 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 20 \ \text{ml}                                                                        &:& 8 \\\\
= & 1                     &:& \dfrac {500} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {150} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {20} {8}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {8} {8} \\\\
= & 1 \times 20           &:& \dfrac {500 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {150 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {20 \times 20} {8}                                                             &:& \dfrac {8 \times 20} {8} \\\\
= & 20                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 250 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 75 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 10 \times 5 \times 4} {4 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 20} {\cancel 8} \\\\
= & 20                    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 250 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 10 \times 5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2}   &:& 20 \\\\
= & 20                    &:& 250 \times 5                                                                           &:& 75 \times 5                                                                            &:& 10 \times 5                                                                           &:& 20 \\\\
= & 20                    &:& 1250 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 375 \ \text{g}                                                                         &:& 50 \ \text{ml}                                                                        &:& 20
\end{array}
$

<div class='question question'>

Jasper is making fairy cakes.   

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-19-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-19-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2012--group-2/section-1-question-19-00.png")

A recipe to make $24$ fairy cakes requires the following
ingredients: 



|$230 \ \text{grams}$| flour  |
|:------------------:|:------:|
|$230 \ \text{grams}$| butter |
|$230 \ \text{grams}$| sugar  |
|$4$                 | eggs   |

</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much flour is needed to make $12$ fairy cakes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Flour needed}       &= \dfrac {230} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {115 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {115 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                          &= 115 \ \text{grams}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
24 \ \text{cakes} : 230 \ \text{grams}              &= 12 \ \text{cakes} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{24} {230}                                &= \dfrac {12} {a} \\\\
24 \times a                                     &= 12 \times 230 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 230} {24} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 2 \times 115} {2 \times 12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times 115} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                               &= 115 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 115 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fairy cakes}                                  &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 24                                                  &:& 230 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & \dfrac {24} {2}                                     &:& \dfrac{230} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {2 \times 12} {2}                            &:& \dfrac{2 \times 115} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 12} {\cancel 2}            &:& \dfrac{\cancel 2 \times 115} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 12                                                  &:& 115 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 115 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$115 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                          &= \dfrac {115 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {115 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                          &= 115 \ \text{grams}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
