<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jasper decides to make $36$ fairy cakes.

Work out the amount of each ingredient which Jasper needs.

|$\ldots \text{grams}$| flour  |
|:------------------:|:------:|
|$\ldots \text{grams}$| butter |
|$\ldots \text{grams}$| sugar  |
|$\ldots$                 | eggs   |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fairy cakes} &:& \text{flour}                                                                                 &:& \text{eggs}                                                                                  &:& \text{butter}                                                                                  &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 24                 &:& 230 \ \text{g}                                                                               &:& 4                                                                                            &:& 230 \ \text{g}                                                                                 &:& 230 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                  &:& \dfrac {230} {24}                                                                            &:& \dfrac {4} {24}                                                                              &:& \dfrac {230} {24}                                                                              &:& \dfrac {230} {24} \\\\
= & 1 \times 36        &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24}                                                                  &:& \dfrac {4 \times 36} {24}                                                                    &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24}                                                                    &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                         &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                         &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}     &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}     &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& 115 \times 3                                                                                 &:& 2 \times 3                                                                                   &:& 115 \times 3                                                                                   &:& 115 \times 3 \\\\
= & 36                 &:& 345 \ \text{g}                                                                               &:& 6                                                                                            &:& 345 \ \text{g}                                                                                 &:& 345 \ \text{g} \\\\
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Flour $=$ $345 \ \text{grams}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Eggs $=$ $6$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Butter $=$ $345 \ \text{grams}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Sugar $=$ $345 \ \text{grams}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fairy cakes} &:& \text{flour}                                                                                 &:& \text{eggs}                                                                                  &:& \text{butter}                                                                                  &:& \text{sugar} \\\\
= & 24                 &:& 230 \ \text{g}                                                                               &:& 4                                                                                            &:& 230 \ \text{g}                                                                                 &:& 230 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                  &:& \dfrac {230} {24}                                                                            &:& \dfrac {4} {24}                                                                              &:& \dfrac {230} {24}                                                                              &:& \dfrac {230} {24} \\\\
= & 1 \times 36        &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24}                                                                  &:& \dfrac {4 \times 36} {24}                                                                    &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24}                                                                    &:& \dfrac {230 \times 36} {24} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                         &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2}                                         &:& \dfrac {115 \times 2 \times 12 \times 3} {12 \times 2} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}     &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2}     &:& \dfrac {115 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12} \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 36                 &:& 115 \times 3                                                                                 &:& 2 \times 3                                                                                   &:& 115 \times 3                                                                                   &:& 115 \times 3 \\\\
= & 36                 &:& 345 \ \text{g}                                                                               &:& 6                                                                                            &:& 345 \ \text{g}                                                                                 &:& 345 \ \text{g} \\\\
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The chocolate icing for $24$ cakes uses $60 \ \text{grams}$ of cocoa 
powder. Jasper decides to put chocolate icing on only half of his $36$ fairy cakes.

How much cocoa powder does he use?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of cocoa powder used $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cakes iced}     &= \dfrac {36} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
24 \ \text{cakes} : 60 \ \text{grams}                     &= 18 \ \text{cakes} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{24} {60}                                           &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
24 \times a                                               &= 18 \times 60 \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {18 \times 60} {24} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {9 \times 2 \times 5 \times 12} {2 \times 12} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel {12}} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                                         &= 9 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                         &= 45 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Cocoa powder used $= 45 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cakes iced}     &= \dfrac {36} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cakes}          &:& \text{cocoa powder} \\\\
= & 24                    &:& 60 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                     &:& \dfrac {60} {24} \\\\
= & 1 \times 18           &:& \dfrac{60 \times 18} {24} \\\\
= & 18                    &:& \dfrac{12 \times 5 \times 9 \times 2} {2 \times 12} \\\\
= & 18                    &:& \dfrac{\cancel {12} \times 5 \times 9 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {12}} \\\\
= & 18                    &:& 5 \times 9 \\\\
= & 18                    &:& 45 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Cocoa powder used $= 45 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$45 \ \text{grams}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of cocoa powder used $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cakes iced}     &= \dfrac {36} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
24 \ \text{cakes} : 60 \ \text{grams}                     &= 18 \ \text{cakes} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{24} {60}                                           &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
24 \times a                                               &= 18 \times 60 \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {18 \times 60} {24} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {9 \times 2 \times 5 \times 12} {2 \times 12} \\\\
a                                                         &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel {12}} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                                         &= 9 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                         &= 45 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cocoa powder used $= 45 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A recipe for $18$ oatcakes needs the following ingredients:

$140 \ \text{grams}$ oatmeal

$140 \ \text{grams}$ porridge oats

$10$ twists of black pepper

$80 \ \text{millilitres}$ olive oil

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

James wants to make $9$ oatcakes.

What mass of oatmeal does James need to use?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the oatmeals needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
18 \ \text{oatcakes} : 140 \ \text{grams}           &= 9 \ \text{oatcakes} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{18} {140}                                &= \dfrac {9} {a} \\\\
18 \times a                                     &= 9 \times 140 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 140} {18} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 2 \times 70} {2 \times 9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times \cancel 2 \times 70} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 70 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Oatmeal needed $= 70 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{oatcakes}                           &:& \text{oatmeals} \\\\
= & 18                                        &:& 140 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & \dfrac {18} {2}                           &:& \dfrac {140} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {2 \times 9} {2}                   &:& \dfrac{70 \times 2} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 9} {\cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac{70 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 9                                         &:& 70 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Oatmeal needed $= 70 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$70 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the oatmeals needed $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
18 \ \text{oatcakes} : 140 \ \text{grams}           &= 9 \ \text{oatcakes} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{18} {140}                                &= \dfrac {9} {a} \\\\
18 \times a                                     &= 9 \times 140 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 140} {18} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 2 \times 70} {2 \times 9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times \cancel 2 \times 70} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 70 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Oatmeal needed $= 70 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>

= & \dfrac {18} {2}                           &:& \dfrac {140} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {2 \times 9} {2}                   &:& \dfrac{70 \times 2} {2} \\\\
= & \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 9} {\cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac{70 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 9                                         &:& 70 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Oatmeal needed $= 70 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Sarah has only $400 \ \text{millilitres}$ of olive oil.

She has plenty of all the other ingredients.

What is the largest number of oatcakes which Sarah can make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the largest number of oatcakes Sarah can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
80 \ \text{ml} : 18 \ \text{oatcakes}           &= 400 \ \text{ml} : a \ \text{oatcakes} \\\\
\dfrac{80} {18}                                 &= \dfrac {400} {a} \\\\
80 \times a                                     &= 400 \times 18 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {400 \times 18} {80} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {80 \times 5 \times 18} {80} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {80} \times 5 \times 18} {\cancel {80}} \\\\
a                                               &= 5 \times 18 \\\\
a                                               &= 90
\end{aligned}
$

Largest number of oatcakes $= 90$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{olive oil}      &:& \text{oatcakes} \\\\
= & 80 \ \text{ml}        &:& 18 \\\\
= & 80 \times 5           &:& 18 \times 5 \\\\
= & 400 \ \text{ml}       &:& 90
\end{array}
$

Largest number of oatcakes $= 90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the largest number of oatcakes Sarah can make $= a$

a                                               &= \dfrac {80 \times 5 \times 18} {80} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {80} \times 5 \times 18} {\cancel {80}} \\\\
a                                               &= 5 \times 18 \\\\
a                                               &= 90
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Fill in the ingredients required to make $27$ oatcakes.

$\ldots$ $\text{grams}$ oatmeal

$\ldots$ $\text{grams}$ porridge oats

$\ldots$ twists of black pepper

$\ldots$ $\text{millilitres}$ olive oil

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{oatcake}    &:& \text{oatmeal}                                                                          &:& \text{porridge oats}                                                                     &:& \text{black pepper}                                                                   &:& \text{olive oil} \\\\
= & 18                &:& 140 \ \text{g}                                                                          &:& 140 \ \text{g}                                                                           &:& 10 \ \text{twists}                                                                    &:& 80 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {140} {18}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {140} {18}                                                                        &:& \dfrac {10} {18}                                                                      &:& \dfrac {80} {18} \\\\
= & 1 \times 27       &:& \dfrac {140 \times 27} {18}                                                             &:& \dfrac {140 \times 27} {18}                                                              &:& \dfrac {10 \times 27} {18}                                                            &:& \dfrac {80 \times 27} {18} \\\\
= & 27                &:& \dfrac {2 \times 70 \times 3 \times 9} {2 \times 9}                                     &:& \dfrac {2 \times 70 \times 3 \times 9} {2 \times 9}                                      &:& \dfrac {2 \times 5 \times 3 \times 9} {2 \times 9}                                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 40 \times 3 \times 9} {2 \times 9} \\\\
= & 27                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 70 \times 3 \times \cancel 9} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9}     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 70 \times 3 \times \cancel 9} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9}      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 5 \times 3 \times \cancel 9} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9}    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 40 \times 3 \times \cancel 9} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9} \\\\
= & 27                &:& 70 \times 3                                                                             &:& 70 \times 3                                                                              &:& 5 \times 3                                                                            &:& 40 \times 3 \\\\
= & 27                &:& 210 \ \text{g}                                                                          &:& 210 \ \text{g}                                                                           &:& 15 \ \text{twists}                                                                    &:& 120 \ \text{ml} \\\\
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Oatmeal $=$ $210 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Porridge Oats $=$ $210 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Black Pepper $=$ $15 \ \text{twists}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Olive Oil $=$ $120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{oatcake}    &:& \text{oatmeal}                                                                          &:& \text{porridge oats}                                                                     &:& \text{black pepper}                                                                   &:& \text{olive oil} \\\\
= & 18                &:& 140 \ \text{g}                                                                          &:& 140 \ \text{g}                                                                           &:& 10 \ \text{twists}                                                                    &:& 80 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {140} {18}                                                                       &:& \dfrac {140} {18}                                                                        &:& \dfrac {10} {18}                                                                      &:& \dfrac {80} {18} \\\\
= & 1 \times 27       &:& \dfrac {140 \times 27} {18}                                                             &:& \dfrac {140 \times 27} {18}                                                              &:& \dfrac {10 \times 27} {18}                                                            &:& \dfrac {80 \times 27} {18} \\\\
= & 27                &:& \dfrac {2 \times 70 \times 3 \times 9} {2 \times 9}                                     &:& \dfrac {2 \times 70 \times 3 \times 9} {2 \times 9}                                      &:& \dfrac {2 \times 5 \times 3 \times 9} {2 \times 9}                                    &:& \dfrac {2 \times 40 \times 3 \times 9} {2 \times 9} \\\\
= & 27                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 70 \times 3 \times \cancel 9} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9}     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 70 \times 3 \times \cancel 9} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9}      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 5 \times 3 \times \cancel 9} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9}    &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 40 \times 3 \times \cancel 9} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 9} \\\\
= & 27                &:& 70 \times 3                                                                             &:& 70 \times 3                                                                              &:& 5 \times 3                                                                            &:& 40 \times 3 \\\\
= & 27                &:& 210 \ \text{g}                                                                          &:& 210 \ \text{g}                                                                           &:& 15 \ \text{twists}                                                                    &:& 120 \ \text{ml} \\\\
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

Mabel found her grandmother's recipe to make scones. 

Here are the ingredients to make $12$ scones:

To make $12$ scones

$200 \ \text{g}$ self-raising flour

pinch of salt

$60 \ \text{g}$ butter

$30 \ \text{g}$ caster sugar

$150 \ \text{ml}$ milk

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If Mabel wants to make $18$ scones, 

How many grams of self raising flour will she need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{scones} : 200 \ \text{grams}             &= 18 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {200}                                &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 18 \times 200 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {18 \times 200} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 6 \times 2 \times 100} {2 \times 6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 6 \times \cancel 2 \times 100} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 100 \\\\
a                                               &= 300 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 300 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{scones}                           &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 200 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {200} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 18                             &:& \dfrac {200 \times 18} {12} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {2 \times 100 \times 6 \times 3} {6 \times 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 100 \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 100 \times 3 \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 300 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 300 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$300 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

How many grams of caster sugar will she need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of caster sugar needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{scones} : 30 \ \text{grams}              &= 18 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {30}                                 &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 18 \times 30 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {18 \times 30} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 6 \times 2 \times 15} {2 \times 6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 6 \times \cancel 2 \times 15} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 15 \\\\
a                                               &= 45 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Caster sugar needed $= 45 \ \text{g}$

</div>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{scones}                           &:& \text{caster sugar} \\\\

= & 1 \times 18                             &:& \dfrac {30 \times 18} {12} \\\\

= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 15 \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 15 \times 3 \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 45 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Caster sugar needed $= 45 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Mabel only has $1.5 \ \text{kg}$ of flour, but has plenty of all the other ingredients.

What is the largest number of scones she can make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the largest number of scones she can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
200 \ \text{g} : 12 \ \text{scones}             &= 1.5 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{scones} \\\\
\dfrac{200 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1.5 \ \text{kg}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{200 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1500 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
200 \times a                                    &= 1500 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1500 \times 12} {200} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {100 \times 15 \times 2 \times 6} {2 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {100} \times 15 \times \cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
a                                               &= 15 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 90
\end{aligned}
$

Maximum scones $= 90$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flour}                            &:& \text{scones} \\\\
= & 200 \ \text{g}                          &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{g}                            &:& \dfrac {12} {200} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1500 \ \text{g}                &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1500} {200} \\\\
= & 1.5 \ \text{kg}                         &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1500} {200} \\\\
= & 1.5                                     &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1500} {200} \\\\
= & 1.5                                     &:& \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times 100 \times 15} {2 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1.5                                     &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 6 \times \cancel {100} \times 15} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
= & 1.5                                     &:& 6 \times 15 \\\\
= & 1.5 \ \text{kg}                         &:& 90
\end{array}
$

Maximum scones $= 90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$90$

</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

Helen's recipe for $12$ waffles needs the following ingredients:

$250 \ \text{grams}$ plain flour

$2$ tablespoons caster sugar

$2$ eggs

$1$ tablespoon baking powder

$420 \ \text{millimeters}$ milk

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What mass of plain flour does Helen need to use in order to make $18$ waffles?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the mass of flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{waffles} : 250 \ \text{grams}            &= 18 \ \text{waffles} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {250}                                &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 18 \times 250 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {18 \times 250} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 6 \times 2 \times 125} {2 \times 6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 6 \times \cancel 2 \times 125} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 125 \\\\
a                                               &= 375 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 375 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{waffles}                          &:& \text{plain flour} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 250 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {250} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 18                             &:& \dfrac {250 \times 18} {12} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {2 \times 125 \times 6 \times 3} {6 \times 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 125 \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 125 \times 3 \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 375 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 375 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$375 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the mass of flour needed $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{waffles} : 250 \ \text{grams}            &= 18 \ \text{waffles} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {250}                                &= \dfrac {18} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 18 \times 250 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {18 \times 250} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 6 \times 2 \times 125} {2 \times 6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 6 \times \cancel 2 \times 125} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 125 \\\\
a                                               &= 375 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Flour needed $= 375 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>

= & 12                                      &:& 250 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {250} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 18                             &:& \dfrac {250 \times 18} {12} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {2 \times 125 \times 6 \times 3} {6 \times 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 125 \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel 6 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 125 \times 3 \\\\
= & 18                                      &:& 375 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Flour needed $= 375 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Helen has only $5$ eggs. She has plenty of all the other ingredients.

What is the largest number of waffles which Helen can make if she has only $5$ eggs?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the largest number of waffles she can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{eggs} : 12 \ \text{waffles}           &= 5 \ \text{eggs} : a \ \text{waffles} \\\\
\dfrac{2} {12}                                  &= \dfrac {5} {a} \\\\
2 \times a                                      &= 5 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 6 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 6 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

Maximum waffles $= 30$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{eggs}                            &:& \text{waffles} \\\\
= & 2                                      &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1                                      &:& \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
= & 1 \times 5                             &:& \dfrac {12 \times 5} {2} \\\\
= & 5                                      &:& \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times 5} {2} \\\\
= & 5                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 6 \times 5} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 5                                      &:& 6 \times 5 \\\\
= & 5                                      &:& 30
\end{array}
$

Maximum waffles $= 30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the largest number of waffles she can make $= a$

<div class='question subquestion'>

Katy has a different recipe for making $12$ waffles.

Her recipe requires $13 \ \text{ounces}$ of plain flour.

Given that $1 \ \text{ounce}$ is approximately equal to $25 \ \text{grams}$, 
work out how much more flour in grams is required for Katy's recipe.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Flour required}                         &= 13 \times 25 \\\\
                                              &= 325 \ \text{g} \\\\
\text{Difference}                             &= 325 - 250 \\\\
                                              &= 75 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>

$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

flour

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{muffins} : 300 \ \text{grams}            &= 9 \ \text{muffins} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {300}                                &= \dfrac {9} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 9 \times 300 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 300} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 12 \times 25} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {12} \times 25} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 25 \\\\
a                                               &= 225 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 225 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{muffins}                          &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 300 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {300} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 9                                       &:& \dfrac {300 \times 9} {12} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& \dfrac {12 \times 25 \times 9} {12} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 25 \times 9} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& 25 \times 9 \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& 225 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 225 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$225 \ \text{g}$

$

\end{aligned}

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Paul has a one kilogram bag of flour. If he uses the whole kilogram of flour to make blueberry muffins, how many can he make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of muffins he can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
300 \ \text{g} : 12 \ \text{muffins}            &= 1 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{muffins} \\\\
\dfrac{300 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1 \ \text{kg}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{300 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
300 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 12} {300} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 3 \times 4} {3 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times \cancel 3 \times 4} {\cancel 3 \times 1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

Number of muffins $= 40$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flour}                            &:& \text{muffins} \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{g}                          &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{g}                            &:& \dfrac {12} {300} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1000 \ \text{g}                &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1000} {300} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 10 \times 100} {3 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4 \times 10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& 4 \times 10 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                           &:& 40
\end{array}
$

Number of muffins $= 40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of muffins he can make $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
300 \ \text{g} : 12 \ \text{muffins}            &= 1 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{muffins} \\\\
\dfrac{300 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1 \ \text{kg}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{300 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
300 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 12} {300} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 3 \times 4} {3 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times \cancel 3 \times 4} {\cancel 3 \times 1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of muffins $= 40$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flour}                            &:& \text{muffins} \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{g}                          &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{g}                            &:& \dfrac {12} {300} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1000 \ \text{g}                &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1000} {300} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 10 \times 100} {3 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4 \times 10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& 4 \times 10 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                           &:& 40
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of muffins $= 40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate the ingredients needed to make this sorbet for $15$ people.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{people}     &:& \text{water}                                         &:& \text{sugar}                                                                          &:& \text{strawberries}                                  &:& \text{eggs} \\\\
= & 6                 &:& 300 \ \text{ml}                                      &:& 100 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 450 \ \text{g}                                       &:& 2 \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {300} {6}                                     &:& \dfrac {100} {6}                                                                      &:& \dfrac {450} {6}                                     &:& \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 15       &:& \dfrac {300 \times 15} {6}                           &:& \dfrac {100 \times 15} {6}                                                            &:& \dfrac {450 \times 15} {6}                           &:& \dfrac {2 \times 15} {6} \\\\
= & 15                &:& \dfrac {6 \times 50 \times 15} {6}                   &:& \dfrac {50 \times 2 \times 5 \times 3} {3 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {6 \times 75 \times 15} {6}                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 5} {3 \times 2} \\\\
= & 15                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 50 \times 15} {\cancel 6}   &:& \dfrac {50 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 75 \times 15} {\cancel 6}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times 5} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 15                &:& 50 \times 15                                         &:& 50 \times 5                                                                           &:& 75 \times 15                                         &:& 5 \\\\
= & 15                &:& 750 \ \text{ml}                                      &:& 250 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 1125 \ \text{g}                                      &:& 5
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Water $=$ $750 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Sugar $=$ $250 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Strawberries $=$ $1125 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Eggs $=$ $5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{people}     &:& \text{water}                                         &:& \text{sugar}                                                                          &:& \text{strawberries}                                  &:& \text{eggs} \\\\
= & 6                 &:& 300 \ \text{ml}                                      &:& 100 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 450 \ \text{g}                                       &:& 2 \\\\
= & 1                 &:& \dfrac {300} {6}                                     &:& \dfrac {100} {6}                                                                      &:& \dfrac {450} {6}                                     &:& \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 15       &:& \dfrac {300 \times 15} {6}                           &:& \dfrac {100 \times 15} {6}                                                            &:& \dfrac {450 \times 15} {6}                           &:& \dfrac {2 \times 15} {6} \\\\
= & 15                &:& \dfrac {6 \times 50 \times 15} {6}                   &:& \dfrac {50 \times 2 \times 5 \times 3} {3 \times 2}                                   &:& \dfrac {6 \times 75 \times 15} {6}                   &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 5} {3 \times 2} \\\\
= & 15                &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 50 \times 15} {\cancel 6}   &:& \dfrac {50 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 75 \times 15} {\cancel 6}   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 3 \times 5} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 15                &:& 50 \times 15                                         &:& 50 \times 5                                                                           &:& 75 \times 15                                         &:& 5 \\\\
= & 15                &:& 750 \ \text{ml}                                      &:& 250 \ \text{g}                                                                        &:& 1125 \ \text{g}                                      &:& 5
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

For how many people is Lily making this sorbet if she uses exactly $1 \ \text{kilogram}$ of sugar?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of people Lily served sorbet $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
100 \ \text{g} : 6 \ \text{people}              &= 1 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{people} \\\\
\dfrac{100 \ \text{g}} {6}                      &= \dfrac {1 \ \text{kg}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{100 \ \text{g}} {6}                      &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
100 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 6} {100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times 6} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

Number of people $= 60$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{sugar}                               &:& \text{people} \\\\
= & 100 \ \text{g}                             &:& 6 \\\\
= & 100 \times 10                              &:& 6 \times 10 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{g}                            &:& 60 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                              &:& 60
\end{array}
$

Number of people $= 60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of people Lily served sorbet $= a$

\dfrac{100 \ \text{g}} {6}                      &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
100 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 6} {100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times 6} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of people $= 60$
</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
trecipe
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

To make $6$ scones you need the following: 

$120\,\text{g}$ of flour

$70\,\text{g}$ of butter

$150\,\text{ml}$ of milk

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much flour would be needed to make $7$ scones?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
6 \ \text{scones} : 120 \ \text{grams}              &= 7 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{6} {120}                                 &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
6 \times a                                      &= 7 \times 120 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 120} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 6 \times 20} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel 6 \times 20} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 7 \times 20 \\\\
a                                               &= 140 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 140 \ \text{g}$

</div>

= & 6                                       &:& 120 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {120} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 7                              &:& \dfrac {120 \times 7} {6} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {6 \times 20 \times 7} {6} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 20 \times 7} {\cancel 6} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& 20 \times 7 \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& 140 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 140 \ \text{g}$

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much butter is required to make $9$ scones?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of butter needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
6 \ \text{scones} : 70 \ \text{grams}               &= 9 \ \text{scones} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac {6} {70}                                 &= \dfrac {9} {a} \\\\
6 \times a                                      &= 9 \times 70 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 70} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 2 \times 35} {3 \times 2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 3 \times \cancel 2 \times 35} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 35 \\\\
a                                               &= 105 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Butter needed $= 105 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{scones}                           &:& \text{butter} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& 70 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {70} {6} \\\\
= & 1 \times 9                              &:& \dfrac {70 \times 9} {6} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 35 \times 3 \times 3} {3 \times 2} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 35 \times 3 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& 35 \times 3 \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& 105 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Butter needed $= 105 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$105 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the maximum number of scones you can make with $1\, \text{litre}$ of milk?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the maximum number of scones we can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
150 \ \text{ml} : 6 \ \text{scones}             &= 1 \ \text{litre} : a \ \text{scones} \\\\
\dfrac{150 \ \text{ml}} {6}                     &= \dfrac {1 \ \text{litre}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{150 \ \text{ml}} {6}                     &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{ml}} {a} \\\\
150 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 6} {150} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 50 \times 3 \times 2} {3 \times 50} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {50} \times \cancel 3 \times 2} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {50}} \\\\
a                                               &= 20 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

Maximum scones $= 40$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{milk}                             &:& \text{scones} \\\\
= & 150 \ \text{ml}                         &:& 6 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {6} {150} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1000 \ \text{ml}               &:& \dfrac {6 \times 1000} {150} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{litre}                        &:& \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 20 \times 50} {3 \times 50} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 3 \times 20 \times \cancel {50}} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {50}} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& 2 \times 20 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{litre}                        &:& 40
\end{array}
$

Maximum scones $= 40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
